# Feature Implementation Plan
**Feature:** validation-run7 — version 0.3.5, the fix that run 7 must pass before shadow mode can be turned off (`plan-pilot.md` P.5 decision (a), applied to run 6's result on 2026-10-25):
- Check 4's second code counted per joined run of recogniser-split pieces, not per segment (the load);
- a dated, blind, sourced widening of the closing and greeting lists (R3), filtered by pre-written mechanical rules that include a real-speech false-cut check;
- rule v3: a narrow R1 exemption for an unsupported clinical line that is the R1 fact's own misheard word under a review mark, printed beside the verdict without it;
- a fresh blind set `heldout-4`, predictions v3 and pre-registered bands before the build, run 7 as the run of record;
- 0.3.4's owed build of record and install smoke, the review guide's 0.3.4 and 0.3.5 lines, the exit gate's wording.

**Overall Progress:** `81%` (22 of 27 buildable tasks done — 0.2, 0.3, 0.3a, 0.4, 0.4a, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8, 1.1–1.4, 2.1–2.2, 3.1–3.3, and H1–H4 (2026-10-27; review rounds 23–27, the suite green over round 23's test change; Phase 3 and the hardening not yet committed); the 10 `[decision]` entries — all taken on 2026-10-25 — are outside the count, the 0.3.4 convention)

## Goal
Make 0.3.5 the build whose run of record (run 7) can honestly PASS a gate decided BEFORE its predictions, so `plan-pilot.md` P.5's shadow-off question can be put again. Nothing is tuned after run 6: every change is a dated decision taken before predictions v3 are written; rule v1's and rule v2's files, the cue file and every seen script's facts stay untouched (C2). The gate (D5, decided 2026-10-25) is the 50 (fitted; regression evidence) plus the blind `heldout-4`; `heldout`, `heldout-2` and `heldout-3` are run and reported in full.

What run 6 left and what this plan does about each (`plan-validation-run6.md` P.4, ids and counts only):

| Class | Run 6 | Cause | This plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| R3 unbroken next patient | `heldout-3`: 9 of 10 boundaries missed (closing unlisted 3, greeting unlisted 2, both 4); 2 reached the note (`h3-02`, `-17`) — over the ceiling of 1 | The frozen lists (14 closings, 12 greetings, unchanged since 2026-09-28) | D2: a dated, blind, sourced widening under pre-written mechanical acceptance rules |
| The left-out-line load | `heldout-3`: 96 `clinician_line_omitted`, median 4, maximum 10 (band ≤ 9 broken); 91 on pieces of split lines (65 beside a `high_risk_omission` piece, 17 beside a partly covered piece, 6 pieces of a whole-line case, 3 beside a `number_source` piece); worst line → 8 segments, 6 warnings | 0.3.4 D1 judges per SEGMENT | D1: one warning per joined run |
| The unsupported line | `h3-03`: a misheard number, R1 (cleared) AND the set's one unsupported clinical line, which fails with no ceiling (`validation.py:2282`) | Rule v2 clears the fact but fails the same word as a line | D4: rule v3's narrow R1 exemption, printed beside the verdict without it |
| R2 tone only | `heldout-3`: 5 facts + 1 bare modal, reported not gated (D17) | Hedges outside every lexicon | D3: frozen; reported |

### Honest forecast
Written before any measurement and not tuned toward:
- **The widening is the only change that can move R3, and its reach on unseen phrasing is unknown** — the blind hedge widening of 2026-10-23 (16 → 33) still left 5 of 8 `heldout-3` doubts unlisted. Four of the 22 seen misses (`h3-20`, `hld-11`, `hld-12`, `h2-22`'s class — a bare time-of-day word) are excluded by the 0.3.3 D2 rule, not by any source, and stay unreachable. `heldout-4` is written by the same author model whose phrasing motivated the widening (and whose memory the lexicon session draws on, WebSearch being disabled): a hit on it partly tests that model's habits against that model's proposals — reported with an author-model qualifier (D5); Tasks 0.3a and 0.4a are the only real-speech evidence.
- **The load fix changes the COUNT on one review row, not what the clinician sees or clicks:** every piece of a left-out line stays `[flagged]` (C4), and acknowledgement is per code (`ui/note.py:1219` `_acknowledge(code)`, groups per code `ui/models.py:2372`), so one click clears the row before and after. What changes: the row's "(N)" counts left-out LINES, as its copy says; the budget and the band become meaningful. Register rows F-139/F-140 say "the clinician acknowledges each" — inaccurate; corrected at P.3.
- **The recogniser residue is untouched.** One misheard quoted number in `heldout-4` within R1's ceiling passes under D4; two fail; a confidently misheard word on an unmarked line fails whatever is decided. A larger model stays its own plan (Excluded; triggered by a run-7 failure on R1 alone).
- **The 50's ceilings (2 / 2 / 2) stay as fitted** and are not lowered; a lower measured R3 after the widening is reported.
- **Real-speech numbers are over n enrolled kept recordings** (n = 2 at the last measurement): a maximum over two recordings is provisional; the record says n.
- A PASS is an outcome to report, never a target.

### Agreed Scope (Build Now — 0.3.5)
- **Phase 0:** rule v3 as its own dated commit FIRST (D4; 0.2); the measurement (0.3, 0.3a); the blind lexicon session and its mechanical acceptance including the kept-recording false-cut filter, then the dated vocabulary commit (D2; 0.4, 0.4a); the fourth-set plumbing before the folder exists (0.5); predictions v3 (D7; 0.6); `heldout-4`'s pre-registration (0.7) and blind authoring (D6; 0.8).
- **Phase 1:** the joined-run unit in the checker, the chooser and the harness, one commit (D1).
- **Phase 2:** the five-set harness run and report; the harness document.
- **Phase 3:** documents and version 0.3.5; the review guide's 0.3.5 lines; the exit-gate draft (D8).
- **Hardening H1–H5**, then **Phase P:** run 7 on five sets, install and smoke (0.3.4's owed lines too), the register and the P.5 question, the `heldout-4` diagnosis.

### Deferred — Actionable Later
- **The practitioner's own greetings and closings** (deferred since 2026-10-13; asked again 2026-10-25: "Not yet"). A later dated vocabulary decision with a repeated run, under D2's rules 1–5.
  - Why deferred: they cannot be named yet.
  - Intended future outcome: the phrases join the lists in one dated commit.
  - Relevant files / subsystems: `consultation_phrases.py`, `test_vocabulary.py`, the D2 acceptance scratch.
  - Dependencies / prerequisites: shadow use on the installed build.
  - Recommended next action: ask again at the 0.3.6 plan.
  - Risk if deferred: minor — Start per patient is the primary control.
  - Revisit by: when the practitioner can name their phrases.
- Carried from `plan-validation-pass.md`, unchanged and still owned there: the one-click undo of a false boundary; the transcript divider at the possible next patient (its home is the ui-refresh Review screen); the mark pairing a retracted statement with its carried correction; the carry's question-answer hop.
- **The ui-refresh adoption** of D1's `flagged_segments(note, document)` and the one-row-per-line count, with 0.3.4's code and marks — at that branch's merge (Plan Lineage). Risk if deferred: correctness (the Review screen would show stale marks until adopted) · Revisit by: the ui-refresh merge.

### Excluded — Revisit Only If Needed
- **A larger Whisper model / a new model pack** — its own plan. When to revisit: run 7 fails on R1 alone (D18's consequence, carried), or R1 breaches its ceiling on any set.
- **A sealed `heldout-5` authored now beside `heldout-4`** (the simplicity lens; the practitioner 2026-10-25: Accept — not now). Why excluded: the next brief depends on run 7's findings, and an unlinted sealed set can hide an authoring error until it is opened. When to revisit: the 0.3.6 plan, if a blind set is needed again.
- **V2 adjacency as the run unit** (any adjacent same-speaker pieces, no punctuation condition; the practitioner 2026-10-25: V1 only). Why excluded: it merges two unrelated unrouted clinician lines into one warning, and choosing it by the budget would be choosing the unit by which one passes.
- **A second blind widening of the hedge lists** (the practitioner 2026-10-25: keep frozen). When to revisit: never from seen idioms (C3); as its own dated decision if free-phrased doubt stays the main residue after run 7.
- **A one-word closing or greeting among the new phrases** (D2 rule 2). When to revisit: if the live watcher gains the positional rule (a safeguards-plan change).
- **A `replay_kept` flag for the load and boundary counts** instead of the gitignored practitioner-only scratch (Tasks 0.3a, 0.4a): the old-list / new-list comparison is one-off; a shipped-tool change would need its report pins and documents. Revisit if the counts are wanted in every replay.
- **A new `NoteWarning` field, a per-warning line number in copy, folding into `high_risk_omission`, removing D1's exclusion of already-flagged lines** (0.3.4's exclusions, unchanged); **a time-gap or speaker-change boundary signal** (unexercisable on the synthetic sets — turns are 600 ms apart and the next patient shares the patient's voice slot); **lowering `BOUNDARY_SEGMENTS`**; **any edit to rule v1's or rule v2's file, the cue file or the seen scripts' facts** (C2); **an eleventh walk flag for a "Silent (0.3.4 predicate)" column** (D1: re-derived and pinned as a subset instead).

### Accepted Assumptions — Revalidate Later
- The practitioner presses Start per patient from that patient's Cliniko note (stated 2026-10-13, 2026-10-22), so the boundary is a backstop. Revalidate if shadow use shows a forgotten switch.
- `3249af0..fb385ce` touches no harness or note code (`git log` 2026-10-25: documents, tests, the register, CI), so Task 0.3's premise run may be taken at HEAD before Phase 1 and must reproduce run 6's reports. Revalidate by the premise check itself.
- The agent shell may see the developer models (the 0.3.4 plan's Task 0.12 re-transcription did); the synthetic audio pass is agent-run if it does and practitioner-run if not (`docs/lessons.md`, MSIX). The kept-recording scripts are practitioner-only whatever the shell sees (C1).
- There are at least 2 consented kept recordings with an enrolled speaker (n = 2 on 2026-10-23). With n < 3 the budget's real-speech line is recorded as provisional, never skipped.
- `heldout-3`'s brief survives the leak check against the widened lists but for its set number, task, date and id prefix: it names "see you next week" (already listed), "welcome" (excluded by rule) and "morning stiffness" / "evening pain" (negatives). Revalidated by Task 0.8's leak check on the EDITED brief.

### Key Design Decisions
D1–D8 below. The practitioner's decisions, all 2026-10-25, in chat, each the recommended option: a blind sourced widening of the closing and greeting lists (D2); the hedge lists frozen (D3); the narrow R1 exemption, disclosed (D4); `heldout-4` by the same isolated `claude -p` method (D6); the budget governs the review row's count (D1, budget); V1 only as the unit (D1); the gate = the 50 + `heldout-4` (D5); no sealed `heldout-5` (Excluded); no own phrases yet (Deferred); executor tier entirely premium.

None counts pieces per run or punctuation-vs-bare-cut.
- Version 0.3.4 in six fields: `desktop/pyproject.toml:7`, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/__init__.py:3`, `extension/package.json:4`, `extension/package-lock.json:3, 9`, `extension/src/manifest.ts:29` (`packaging/scribe.iss` takes `{#AppVersion}` from `build-release.py:163-168`; `BUILD-INFO.txt` is generated).

### Codebase Integration Notes
- Checks are pure functions of `(note, document, config)` run at every compose; a changed count needs no stored field (`note.enc`'s `note_warnings` list just holds fewer entries). A SAVED note reopened from the Unreviewed section shows its stored warnings unchanged (0.3.4's peer round 5).
- `ui/models.py` is imported by `validation.py` (no Qt at module level) and `note_check` is Qt-free; `note.py` is the leaf both import, so `covered_words` (with `_resolved_coords` / `_words_for_coords`) lives there and `note_check` re-exports the names its five call sites and `validation.py`'s three still use.
- The harness evaluates through the product's own functions; `warned` must read the same run the product counts by (C5), else the gate measures a different unit than the clinician sees.
- The live new-consultation warning and the boundary share the lists but not the positional rule (`positional=False` live): a one-word phrase would behave differently in the two — the reason for D2 rule 2.
- `test_validation_set.py:1065-1090` reads the lists live against the digest-pinned `ENROLMENT_PASSAGE` (:1034): the passage cannot change (its WAV is run 6's enrolment audio), so a candidate phrase present in it is rejected, never the passage edited.
- The blind runner's argument names only the log; the child lints every set in the fixture table (`lint-held-out-set.py:17-18`), so CI stays green when `h4-` scripts land, but `ci.yml:69`'s argument and comment should read `heldout-4`.

### External / API Findings
None. No Cliniko, network, model or dependency change.

## Planned Workflow Summary

### Flow 1 — A left-out line the recogniser split
The clinician says one advice sentence; the recogniser writes it as three segments, the first two ending in a comma. Today (0.3.4): three "A line you said is not in the note" warnings. 0.3.5: ONE, anchored on the first piece; all three pieces `[flagged]` in the chooser; "Show flagged lines only" and Add line reach each; one click clears the row as before.

### Flow 2 — A next patient greeted in other words
The clinician closes with a phrase the 2026-09-28 list lacks and welcomes the next person with another. Today: no boundary; the next patient's cued line reaches the note. 0.3.5, if both phrases entered by D2: Check 6's warning, the note stops, the chooser marks the tail `[next patient?]`; the live new-consultation warning also fires on the pair (disclosed).

### Flow 3 — A misheard number in a quoted line (the harness)
The recogniser writes a wrong number in a patient's quoted line; the note marks it `[check: number]`. Rule v2: the fact is R1, the line fails the encounter. Rule v3: the line is excused when the only unsupported structured word in it is that R1 fact's own misrendering under the mark; the report prints the verdict with and without the exemption, and the line is a register row either way.

### Flow 4 — The practitioner's runs
0.2 rule v3 committed → 0.3 / 0.3a measurement (synthetic; kept, integers) → 0.4 the lexicon session, the mechanical acceptance incl. 0.4a's kept-recording false-cut filter → the vocabulary commit → 0.5 the fourth-set plumbing → 0.6 predictions v3 → 0.7 the pre-registration commit → 0.8 `heldout-4` blind → Phases 1–3, H1–H4 → P.1 run 7 (five sets) → H5 → P.2 install + smoke → P.3 → P.4 the `heldout-4` diagnosis.

## Design Decisions

### D1 — Check 4's second code is judged per JOINED RUN (decided 2026-10-25: V1 only; the budget governs the review row's count)
- **The unit.** A *joined run* is a maximal run of adjacent segments, within the caller's cut, where each pair `join(k, k+1)` holds: `k + 1 < end`; the same `speaker` label; `ends_mid_sentence(seg[k].transcript_words)` (`note.py:1760` — a trailing comma, semicolon, colon, en/em dash; a bare cut is NOT a join, the existing named residue); `segment_has_text(seg[k+1])` (a textless segment never joins, forward or backward — the `segment_hedged` precedent); and both wholly uncovered (no `(k, w)` in `covered`). New helper `note.joined_run(document, i, *, covered, end) -> range`: the maximal run CONTAINING `i` (extend left while `join(k-1, k)`, right while `join(k, k+1)`, clamped to `[0, end)`) — ONE implementation (C5). A partly covered segment, a speaker change, a textless segment or the cut breaks a run; a run that spans the cut is truncated, never dropped (pinned). `covered` comes from `note.covered_words(note, document)` — moved from `note_check._covered_words` with `_resolved_coords` and `_words_for_coords`, re-exported by `note_check` for its five call sites and `validation.py`'s three.
- **The code.** `note_check.clinician_line_omissions` iterates `i = 0; while i < end: run = joined_run(...); …; i = run.stop` (runs partition `[0, end)`) and draws ONE `clinician_line_omitted` per run whose segments are all spoken by the confirmed clinician, wholly uncovered, with a non-suspect content token somewhere in the run, when NO segment of the run is in `already` (the `high_risk_omission` set, `:1715-1719` — one remedy, add the line); anchored `SourceCoords(run.start, 0, len(words) - 1)` (single-segment, no new field — `extra="forbid"`, 0.3.3 would refuse the note). A length-1 run is the 0.3.4 case unchanged. `high_risk_omission` is unchanged.
- **The chooser (C4, load-bearing).** `ui/models.flagged_segments(note, document)` STARTS from every omission anchor of either code — exactly today's set, the tail included (`high_risk_omission` is emitted over the whole transcript, `note_check.py:1673`, so an anchor at or past the boundary exists and must keep its mark; peer round 1 PR-MED-001) — and then ADDS, for every such anchor that lies before the cut (`next_patient_segment(note.note_warnings)`), was spoken by the confirmed clinician and is wholly uncovered, the anchor's whole joined run (the same `joined_run`); a patient's `high_risk_omission`, a partly covered clinician anchor, or any anchor at or past the cut marks its own segment only, as today. So the 65 pieces `[flagged]` under 0.3.4 by their own anchors stay `[flagged]` under 0.3.5 by the run — pinned by a both-rules comparison over a fixture matrix of split documents, with 0.3.4's predicate written as a test ORACLE shared with the harness's re-derivation (below). Caller `ui/note.py:1267-1271` (guard a None document); the count line counts the same rows.
- **The harness (same commit).** `credited_segments` (`validation.py:1816-1835`) becomes the union of the joined runs of the qualifying anchors (clinician-spoken, wholly uncovered, before the boundary) — still a subset of the chooser's `[flagged]` lines. **Run 6's reading is RE-DERIVED, not stored:** the 0.3.4 credited set = the segments 0.3.4's per-segment predicate would anchor (wholly uncovered confirmed-clinician segments with a non-suspect content token, not in `omission_segments`, before the boundary) plus the `high_risk_omission` anchors that qualify — computed from the document as `hedged` is (:1844-1857), never read from the 0.3.5 note's warnings (which anchor fewer segments). The chain `credited_0.3.5 ⊇ credited_0.3.4 ⊇ credited_0.3.3's anchors` holds by construction (a 0.3.4-credited segment is a one-segment qualifying run) and is asserted per encounter; the residue section prints one line "segments credited by the run only: N" and the count of facts credited only by the run (a second walk over a copy of the word infos, no walk flag). Both reach the report through two validated `EncounterMetrics` counts (peer round 2 PR-MED-006 — `_residue_section` receives outcomes only, `validation.py:3141-3149`): `segments_credited_by_run_only: int = 0` = |credited_0.3.5 − credited_0.3.4|, and `facts_credited_by_run_only: int = 0` = the material facts whose `silent_omission` FLIPS between two walks: the fact's real walk, and a second `_fact_end_states` call over a copy of the `_WordInfo` list whose `warned` is computed from the 0.3.4 credited set — the same rule as the real one (any diagonal edge, matched or not, `validation.py:1324-1331`; a suspect word never credited, `:1879-1881`; the `warned_v033` path kept), the same conservative worst outcome over co-optimal alignments; no new walk flag or column (peer round 3 PR-MED-009: a lookup over matching edges alone miscounts a fact already warned through a substitution on an old-credited piece); reported only, read by no rule; each `_require_count`ed, `facts_credited_by_run_only` ≤ the material facts, both `0` when `reference is None`; the residue line sums them over the measured encounters (`-` with none measured, the PR-LOW-016 convention). No new column: the 28-column pins and `_CLUSTERING_BASELINE_C5F3179` stand. The report header cites the amendment by date. `[2026-10-26, as built, review round 14 LOW-003/LOW-004]` "Asserted per encounter" is a TEST assertion (the matrix and every three-piece tail, `fixtures.joined_runs.every_small_tail`), not a run-time raise; and `facts_credited_by_run_only` is 0 by construction on every note (Task 1.3's disposition line), so its walk is a tripwire.
- **The budget (decision 0.1, 2026-10-25 (a)):** median ≤ 2, maximum ≤ 5 `clinician_line_omitted` per real consultation over n enrolled kept recordings, recorded met or unmet with n (provisional at n < 3), the `[flagged]`-line count beside it. V1 is the unit on its merits; the budget never chooses the unit.
- **Rejected:** V2 adjacency; a `warned_v034` walk flag and column (six structures for a number the superset pin and one line give); a UI-only row count (`summarise_warnings` has no document — the displayed "(N)" would disagree with the saved note and with 0.3.3's `unknown_review_warning` row); a harness-only count (cannot be taken on real speech, where the budget lives); the 0.3.4 exclusions (a new field, line numbers in copy, folding into `high_risk_omission`, removing the already-flagged exclusion).
- **Rollback.** Nothing new on disk; fewer warnings of a code 0.3.4 already wrote. "Rolling back below 0.3.5" says 0.3.4's section applies from this build (0.3.3 reads the code as `unknown_review_warning`, `note.py:787-809`; its pin, 0.3.4 Task 1.3, is the rollback control — no two-version install smoke), and names the vocabulary: a note 0.3.3 regenerates may include lines 0.3.5 would withhold at a boundary 0.3.3's lists do not find.

### D2 — A dated, blind, sourced widening of the closing and greeting lists (decided 2026-10-25)
- **Method = the 0.3.4 Task 0.9 shape under the held-out author's isolation:** a `claude -p` session in an empty folder outside the repository (`--restricted --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --disallowedTools WebSearch Agent --permission-mode acceptEdits`, stream-json log kept as the record, the user name redacted) given ONLY the selection brief: propose closings and greetings from NAMED published sources — conversation analysis of openings and closings (Schegloff & Sacks 1973; Schegloff 1968, 1986; Button 1987), medical-visit openings and closings (Robinson 1998, 2001; Heritage & Robinson 2006; West 2006), Australian English usage (the Australian National Dictionary; Macquarie), ESL grammars (Carter, McCarthy, Mark & O'Keeffe 2011, "Greetings and farewells") — one source per phrase. WebSearch is disabled, so the sources come from the model's memory: the contamination note of 0.3.4's Task 0.9 applies and is written again. The session never sees the failing scripts, the register or this plan; a phrase known from the failures (P.4 names none by text) enters only if a source holds it, membership recorded either way.
- **Acceptance rules, written here BEFORE the session, applied mechanically to the candidate SET, iterated to a fixpoint (later-proposed first), recorded per phrase as accepted / rejected-with-reason (class and count, never a failing script's text):**
  1. whole-word form (`phrase_tokens`), unique, in neither list; the 0.3.3 D2 exclusions as the pin reads them (`test_vocabulary.py:100-111`): no phrase that IS a bare time-of-day word (`morning`, `afternoon`, `evening`, `night`, `day`) in either list, "welcome" absent as a token across both, "how are you going" absent;
  2. no NEW one-word phrase of either kind (the live watcher reads `positional=False`, `consultation_phrases.py:221`, so a one-word greeting would fire the live warning anywhere; the near-miss pins `test_vocabulary.py:132-143` stay); the existing "hello", "bye", "goodbye" are untouched — "cheers" and the like are excluded by rule, a conservative choice, recorded;
  3. not a hedge (`HEDGE_PHRASES`, `REFERENCE_HEDGES`), not a correction marker, not present in `validation_set.ENROLMENT_PASSAGE` (the enforcing structure is `test_validation_set.py:1065-1090`, run before acceptance; the passage never changes); a GREETING that is a shipped or practitioner cue phrase is rejected (a cue-greeting would route content); a closing that is a cue is admitted and recorded (the head-case guard reads cues strictly before the closing's segment, so a cue-closing cannot cut itself — "see you next week" is already both);
  4. **the synthetic false-cut filter (a C4 safety filter, not evidence):** under the candidate lists `note_config.next_patient_boundary(reference_document(script), config)` must stay None for every NON-axis script and every axis NEGATIVE of the four seen sets; a boundary there rejects the phrase(s) that create it. On an axis POSITIVE (expected boundary b = the seen script's `HeldOutCase.boundary` where the fixture has a case — `heldout`, `heldout-2`, `heldout-3`, all `cases_written` — and `validation.reference_tail_start(script)` only for the 50, whose six positives have no `HeldOutCase` (`test_validation_scripts.py:1092-1110`; peer round 3 PR-MED-008). The two are NOT interchangeable on a `tails_by_role` set: `heldout-3`'s role tail is its first `next_patient` line, one past the boundary the fixture pins (`h3-02` tail 10, boundary 9; `h3-09` tail 6, boundary 5 — `validation.py:639, 646`, `test_consultation_boundary.py:135-136`; `test_validation_scripts.py:948-953` checks them apart; peer round 4 PR-MED-011), so the role tail stays R3's reference and never rule 4's. A None b is an axis negative or a non-axis script — the same test's b = None case) the candidate boundary is RECORDED (before and after, ids and integers — D14's recorded check) and is never a reason to ACCEPT a phrase, but two outcomes REJECT the phrase(s) that cause them as safety failures (peer round 2 PR-MED-005): a cut EARLIER than b (a false cut of the first patient's own lines — C4, the negatives' failure inside a positive), and a positive that matched b under the current lists and no longer does (later, or None — a regression; `consultation_boundary` breaks at the NEAREST closing, `consultation_phrases.py:272-277`, so a new nearer closing can move a cut later or remove it, not only earlier). A positive that diverged before and still diverges, at a later cut than b or None, is admitted and recorded; its `_PREDICTED_BOUNDARY_DIVERGENCES_BY_SET` entry takes the new frozen-list value in the vocabulary commit, the old value kept in the dated before-record;
  5. **the real-speech false-cut filter (Task 0.4a, practitioner-run, integers):** a kept recording is normally one consultation (Start per patient), but that is an Accepted Assumption, not a fact (peer round 1 PR-MED-004), so a recording is a NEGATIVE only once the PRACTITIONER confirms, per session ordinal and from their own knowledge of that consultation, that it held one patient — the agent never learns why. On a CONFIRMED single-patient recording any boundary the candidate lists find is false, and the phrase(s) that create it are rejected and recorded by class. A recording the practitioner marks as holding a real hand-over, or cannot confirm, is EXCLUDED from rejection decisions: a boundary there is recorded as an integer only (and a found real hand-over is a recorded true positive, never a reason to accept a phrase). The record holds aggregates only: n kept, n confirmed single-patient, n excluded (hand-over / unconfirmed), boundaries on confirmed negatives, rejections by class. The script also prints a labelled SURROGATE of the live new-consultation warning per session under the old and the candidate lists: a fresh `NewConsultationWatcher` fed the replayed document ONE SEGMENT PER `feed()` call in order, printing raised or not (0/1) and the feed ordinal it raised at. It is not the live count and is labelled so (peer round 3 PR-MED-010): the live feed batches are the live segmenter's windows over live text (`transcription.py:2495-2552`), segments carry no batch identity (`:340-348`), the watcher advances one window per `feed()` and raises once per recording (`voice_commands.py:153-170`), and the replayed final transcript differs from the live one (an estimate of the C7 consequence's direction, never its live magnitude). The printed phrase reference is an INDEX into the candidate list (a public constant once committed), never text.
- **One dated commit** (before predictions v3 and before `heldout-4` is authored; C3): the two lists; `BOUNDARY_SEGMENTS` unchanged; the exact pins (`test_vocabulary.py:50-81`, `test_hands_free.py:358-361`); the seen sets' boundary divergence dicts — a case the widening FIXES leaves `_PREDICTED_BOUNDARY_DIVERGENCES_BY_SET` (`test_consultation_boundary.py:112`, which asserts `got != case.boundary` :382) for a dated record `_BOUNDARY_PREDICTIONS_BEFORE_RUN7_0_4` asserted both ways (the 0.3.4 Task 0.9 precedent, `_UNSURE_PREDICTIONS_BEFORE_RUN6_0_9`), a case that still diverges at a CHANGED value takes the new value with the old one in the same dated record (`:380` asserts `got == frozen_list_result`), the count pin :391 lowered, the expected column never changed; the threat model's NEXT PATIENT (:543 counts; residue (a)) and HANDS-FREE paragraphs; `clinician-review-guide.md:123`; `validation-harness.md:194`; `AGENTS.md:138`; the acceptance record (every phrase class accepted / rejected, each rule's counts). Codex peer pass over the diff, as Task 0.9 had. The live warning's copy (`ui/models.py:939-941`) and the side panel name no phrase — no change.
- **Consequence, disclosed (0.3.3 C7: the live warning "changes only by the vocabulary commit"):** `voice_commands.NewConsultationWatcher` warns on more pairs; it never acts; rule 5's surrogate estimates its direction on real speech; its live magnitude stays unmeasured, named in the threat model's HANDS-FREE paragraph. P.2 exercises one accepted pair live (the practitioner speaking both roles).
- **Rejected:** a time-gap or speaker-change signal; lowering `BOUNDARY_SEGMENTS`; frozen lists (R3 stays the blind set's likeliest failure); the practitioner's own phrases now ("Not yet", Deferred).

### D3 — The hedge lists stay frozen (decided 2026-10-25)
`HEDGE_PHRASES` (33) and `REFERENCE_HEDGES` (52) unchanged. Uncertainty on the blind set stays reported, not gated (D17 carried: `heldout-4`'s row has no R2 ceiling); the 50's R2 ceiling 2 stands; a bare modal stays a fail by design (0.3.4 D5/D17). The register's control (the clinician reads the transcript) stands.

### D4 — Rule v3: a narrow R1 exemption for an unsupported clinical line (decided 2026-10-25; the predicate fixed in this plan's commit, C15)
- **Predicate.** Under schema 3 only, an unsupported clinical line (`validation.py:1898-1911`) is EXCUSED when EVERY structured-claim word flagged in it (`is_structured_claim_word` over the line's `flagged` set) is a hypothesis token j such that (i) no reference position i has `insertion(i, j)` — never an inserted word; (ii) every MISMATCHING diagonal edge `diagonal(i, j)` with `reference[i] != hypothesis[j]` (as `possibly_unsupported` reads it, :1160-1164) has i inside an occurrence row range `[first, last]` of a MATERIAL fact of this encounter classed R1 — the fact's OWN misrendering on every optimal alignment that flags j (each optimal path flags j through exactly one edge, so the edge-wise test equals the path-wise one); (iii) `infos[j].review_marked` holds — the word itself under a review mark (stricter than R1's `marked`, which needs SOME aligned word marked). Computed in a SECOND PASS between the facts loop (R1 known at :2002; collect `r1_ranges` from the loop's locals `first` / `last` for material R1 facts and `flagged_by_line` from :1905-1911) and `EncounterMetrics(...)` at :2012; `reference is None` ⇒ no R1 ⇒ excused `()`. `EncounterMetrics` gains `unsupported_lines_excused_by_r1: tuple[tuple[int, int], ...] = ()` — the excused lines' IDENTITIES, not a bare count (peer round 1 PR-MED-003): each entry `(line ordinal, segment index)`, the line ordinal 0-based into `_note_lines`' list (`validation.py:1635-1660`, transcript-provenance assertions in note order — deterministic for a given note) and the segment index the assertion's `source_coords.segment_index`; sorted, unique, every ordinal < `transcript_lines`, `len(...) ≤ unsupported_clinical_lines` (validated in `__post_init__`; the default `()` keeps the test constructors :1760-1773 and the scratch's `EncounterMetrics(**raw)` working). The excused COUNT is always `len(...)`, never stored separately; the residue section prints the members as `<id>/line <ordinal + 1> (segment <index>)` — ids and integers. The raw column is unchanged (rule v1 and v2 read it). **Stated in advance: if `h3-03` is NOT excused under this predicate (its misrendering a substitution plus an insertion, say), that is the result — the predicate is not widened.**
- **The rule's reading.** `_encounter_failures` gains `exempt: bool`: the unsupported-line failure reads raw − excused only when `residues` and the rule exempts; `v1_failures` stays raw. **The schema-3 report prints THREE verdicts on the same outcomes — "rule v3 / rule v3 without the R1 exemption / rule v1"** (the heading :2908-2909 and the amendments paragraph :2913-2929 gain a schema-3 variant), so the cycle's one loosening is visible in run 7's printout; the residue section prints the excused count and members (`id/line`). **Rule v2's verdict comes from the same printout, not a second run** (peer round 2 PR-MED-007): `run_passed` / `rule_failures` / `class_breaches` read a row's ceilings and never its `gate` (`validation.py:2775-2789`; `gate` is read only by the labels, :2863-2865, :3160), and rule v3's rows for `scripts`, `heldout`, `heldout-2`, `heldout-3` copy rule v2's ceilings unchanged — so on those four sets "rule v3 without the R1 exemption" IS rule v2's verdict, and the line says so ("= rule v2's verdict on this set"); on `heldout-4` it says "rule v2 has no row for this set" (`PassRule.set_row` would refuse it, :2131-2134). Pinned: rule v3's four seen rows equal `rule-v2.json`'s in every field but `heldout-3`'s `gate`.
- **Every excused line is a register finding** — a `controlled` row (class: a misheard structured word under a review mark; the control names the mark and the clinician's check of every marked number) regardless of the verdict, never `resolved` by the run (P.3). Worst case stays named: a wrong dose quoted under `[check: number]`, whose control is weakened by the mark's ubiquity (rule v2's "Honest limit").
- **Loosenings, all listed:** (5) the exemption, which reaches ANY structured-claim class (a misheard side or negation under a `low_confidence_source` mark on an R1 fact is excused too); it applies to the 50 as well (0 unsupported lines in run 6, so no verdict moves). Disclosed with D15's (1)–(4) in `validation-harness.md`, the run-of-record entry, and `plan-pilot.md` Constraint 12 as a DATED OVERRIDE LINE in the practitioner's words (the 0.3.4 precedent) — the words: "Narrow R1 exemption (Recommended)", chosen 2026-10-25 in reply to the question quoted on the Key Design Decisions line, with its reasoning (rule v2 is internally inconsistent: R1 clears the fact but the same misheard word fails the encounter as a line; the product's control is the mark the clinician checks). `[2026-10-26, Task 0.2 review round 6 LOW-004]` The Key Design Decisions line quotes no question text, so the dated override lines (`validation-harness.md` "Rule v3", `plan-pilot.md` Constraint 12) quote the practitioner's answer and cite the decision, never an invented question.

Rule v3's pass requirement on the 50 (gate; 2 / 2 / 2, fitted, not lowered) and on `heldout-4` (gate; R1 1 fact, R3 1 encounter, uncertainty reported — `heldout-3`'s row, no looser). `heldout`, `heldout-2`, `heldout-3` reported in full (rows unchanged; `heldout-3` is seen since P.4 — regression evidence only, so not gated). A `heldout-4` pass is ALWAYS reported with D16's scope qualifier (patient facts routed by rule) AND, for R3, the author-model qualifier (Honest forecast).

Bands (reported, outside = a finding, never a rule edit): the new code's load median and maximum under D1 (from Task 0.3's seen-set numbers); the surfaced-doubt rate; the reference-boundary mismatches; R2 any count; boundaries found out of role tails (D2's measure on unseen text — not circular: it counts false and true cuts on text the lists never saw).

### D7 — Predictions v3, then the build
From a scratch over the seen sets (text level from the scripts; audio level from Task 0.3's documents, kept until 0.6 so nothing is re-transcribed), frozen BEFORE the build: for the 50, `heldout`, `heldout-2`, `heldout-3`, EVERY verdict line re-walked — rule v1, rule v2, rule v3 with and without the exemption (the run-6 lesson: a credit that reaches a shared count reaches every verdict reading it) — the boundary per axis script under the widened lists, the new code's total / median / maximum under D1, the exemption's effect, R2 unchanged. The scratch's run function is pinned equal to `note.joined_run` over the fixture matrix in Phase 1; a divergence on a reported band is a finding against the scratch, never the product. Anything outside the predictions at run 7 is a finding, never a rule edit.

## Schema / Data Changes
- **No new stored field.** `clinician_line_omitted` is persisted less often (one per run); every build from 0.3.2 reads it as before (0.3.3 as `unknown_review_warning`).
- **`PassRule` schema 3:** `rule-v3.json` with `unsupported_line_r1_exemption` and a `heldout-4` row; `RULE_SCHEMA_VERSION` 3; schema 1 and 2 files read unchanged.
- **`EncounterMetrics.unsupported_lines_excused_by_r1`** (the excused lines' `(line ordinal, segment index)` identities, default `()`; the count is its length); **`EncounterMetrics.segments_credited_by_run_only` / `facts_credited_by_run_only`** (report-only counts, default `0`; Task 1.3); **`validation.REFERENCE_TAIL_STARTS`** gains the `h4-` rows (Task 0.8); the frozen lists change in one dated commit (Task 0.4); `note.covered_words` / `joined_run` are new public helpers; `HeldOutSet` gains a fourth row.

## Config / Environment / Deployment Impact
- No environment variable, network, model-pack or dependency change.
- Release: 0.3.5 built of record on the H4 commit after P.1; same AppId, in-place upgrade over the installed 0.3.3; install at a pilot-allowed point (never between consultations). The first installed build carrying 0.3.4's new review code: "Rolling back below 0.3.4" applies from it.

## Critical Constraints
1. **C1 — The agent never opens, reads, plays or describes any real session, kept recording, transcript, note, Past-sessions entry or learned config.** Synthetic sets and integer counts only; the kept-recording measurement is a practitioner-run gitignored script (`.cursor/loops/run7-measure-kept.py`) reproducing `replay_kept.main`'s refusal chain, printing session ordinals, integers and phrase INDICES into a candidate list (never text), and a separate entry point from the id-printing synthetic script.
2. **C2 — Rule v1's and rule v2's files, `validation/config/section_cues.json`, and the facts of every seen script never change.** Rule v3 is a NEW file ratified in Task 0.2 before predictions v3; a bad number after a run is a finding (pilot Constraint 12, clinic-smoke D9).
3. **C3 — The closing and greeting lists change in ONE dated commit (Task 0.4) before `heldout-4` is authored and before predictions v3; the hedge lists are frozen.** Any later change is a new dated decision with a repeated run; the exact pins move with it.
4. **C4 — Fail toward inclusion and never silently.** No segment `[flagged]` under 0.3.4 loses its mark (the both-rules pin); the new code never deletes or re-routes; the boundary never deletes; a vocabulary candidate that cuts any seen negative or any kept recording the practitioner confirmed as one patient is rejected (D2 rule 5).
5. **C5 — One function per rule, shared by product and harness:** `note.joined_run`, `note.covered_words`, `note_config.next_patient_boundary`, `note.segment_hedged`, `validation.reference_document`; the harness's `warned` reads the run the product counts by.
6. **C6 — No import of `voice_commands` from `note`, `note_config`, `note_fill` or `note_check`.**
7. **C7 — The live new-consultation warning changes ONLY by the vocabulary commit** (disclosed; its direction on real speech estimated by Task 0.4a's labelled surrogate, its live magnitude unmeasured).
8. **C8 — Nothing per consultation enters a tracked file** (clinic-smoke D12). Run records are ids and counts; kept-recording measurements are aggregates (n, median, max) and per-ordinal integers in the practitioner's console only.
9. **C9 — One `/execute-loop` at a time on `main`; isolation none.** `main`'s version is never lowered.
10. **C10 — Practitioner-run steps run from a normal terminal with the installed app closed.** Nothing checked from an agent shell counts. Any record script that writes into this plan asserts `"\x00" not in text` and `"\r" not in text` first.
11. **C11 — `test_shadow_exits.py`'s counters are unchanged.** No new text widget, clipboard, drag, list, combo box or plaintext kept-recording reader in the package (the kept-recording scratch is outside it).
12. **C12 — `heldout-4` is read by no leg before run 7.** Its only readers are the lints through the runner; the role-derived tails are copied by a script that prints ids and integers; its diagnosis is Task P.4, after the run.
13. **C13 — No cloud sessions.** The findings register stays content-free (month dates, closed vocabularies, phrase classes never a failing script's text; `test_pilot_docs.py`).
14. **C14 — The harness's attribution policy (0.3.4 D11) is unchanged.**
15. **C15 — D4's predicate (i)–(iii) is fixed in the commit that carries this plan, and rule v3 (Task 0.2) is committed BEFORE any alignment of `heldout-3` is re-read (Task 0.3).** If `h3-03` is not excused under it, that is the result.

## Review Findings Log
### Round 1 — 2026-10-25
Source: Codex (codex gpt-6-astra medium) plan peer-review

**PR-MED-001 — Preserve omission anchors beyond the boundary**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:150-152` expands wholly uncovered clinician anchors using a run clamped before the boundary, without preserving anchors outside that interval. However, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note_check.py:1673-1695` emits `high_risk_omission` across the entire transcript, including clinician segments after the boundary; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:2714-2723` currently marks those anchors. Such an anchor cannot belong to the prescribed bounded run, contradicting C4.  
Proposed amendment: Initialise the chooser’s result with every existing omission anchor, expand only eligible anchors inside the cut, and pin a wholly uncovered clinician high-risk anchor at or beyond the boundary.

**PR-MED-002 — Copying rule-v2 rows retains the wrong gate membership**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:182` makes `heldout-3` reported only, but Task 0.2 at `:272` specifies “the four rows as v2”; `validation/rules/rule-v2.json:28` sets `heldout-3.gate` to true. `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:2863-2865` derives reported gate membership directly from these fields.  
Proposed amendment: Explicitly set `heldout-3.gate=false` in rule-v3 only and test that its gate names are exactly `scripts` and `heldout-4`.

**PR-MED-003 — Excused-line identities do not reach the report**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:175` requires excused members as `id/line`, while `:174` and Task 0.2 introduce only an integer count. `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:951-962` carries no corresponding line identities, and `_residue_section` at `:3141-3149` receives outcomes rather than the source lines or alignment needed to reconstruct them.  
Proposed amendment: Retain validated immutable excused-line indices in the result, define their indexing convention, derive or validate the count against them, and test the reported members.

**PR-MED-004 — Kept recordings are not verified negative examples**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:165` and `:276` classify every boundary in a kept recording as false “by definition”, although `:93` explicitly treats Start-per-patient as an assumption that may fail. A genuine handover would therefore reject a correctly detecting candidate and invalidate the false-cut classification.  
Proposed amendment: Require practitioner confirmation that each negative recording contains one encounter, exclude ambiguous or genuine handovers from rejection decisions, and report aggregate eligibility counts without exposing content.

Materiality: 4 build-affecting / 0 record-only / 0 invalid

### Round 2 — 2026-10-25
Source: Codex (codex gpt-6-astra medium) plan peer-review

**PR-MED-005 — Positive scripts can acquire premature cuts without rejection**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:164` excludes axis positives from rejection decisions, but `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/consultation_phrases.py:267-279` returns the first qualifying boundary. Widening can therefore introduce a false cut before a positive script’s real handover. The plan at `:166` handles only fixed divergences, whereas `desktop/tests/test_consultation_boundary.py:379-384` also rejects changed-but-still-wrong boundaries. Recording such a regression alone cannot satisfy the prescribed green-suite gate.  
Proposed amendment: Reject newly premature cuts before positive scripts’ expected boundaries as false-cut safety failures, and specify how changed-but-unfixed divergence predictions are handled.

**PR-MED-006 — Run-only credit counts have no path into the report**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:153` and `:318` promise run-only credited segment and fact counts, but `:202` adds only the unrelated excused-line identities to the result schema. `credited_segments`, alignment information and fact-occurrence ranges remain local to `encounter_metrics` (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:1823`, `:1893-1895`, `:1927-1940`); `_residue_section` receives outcomes without those inputs (`:3141-3149`), and existing `EncounterMetrics` fields (`:951-962`) cannot reconstruct the promised counts. This is another output affected by Round 1 PR-MED-003’s defect class; its excused-line amendment itself is sound.  
Proposed amendment: Define validated result fields carrying both run-only credit counts into the report, specify their fact-alignment counting semantics, and pin zero, positive and aggregate outputs.

**PR-MED-007 — P.1 requires a fourth verdict the specified report cannot supply**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:338` requires v1/v2/v3/v3-without-exemption verdicts for every set, while D4 at `:175` and Task 0.2 at `:305` specify three verdicts. `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:2856-2858` and `:2889-2896` receive one rule; unchanged `validation/rules/rule-v2.json:8-33` has no `heldout-4` row, which `PassRule.set_row` would refuse (`validation.py:2131-2134`). “V3 without exemption” does not supply historical v2 gate membership either.  
Proposed amendment: Specify how v2 comparisons on the four existing sets are obtained from the same outcomes, mark heldout-4’s v2 verdict unavailable, and reconcile P.1 with the three-verdict report.

### Round 3 — 2026-10-25
Source: Codex (codex gpt-6-astra medium) plan peer-review

**PR-MED-008 — The positive-script filter lacks expected boundaries for the 50**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:164` specifies `HeldOutCase.boundary` as the expected boundary for axis positives across all four seen sets. However, `desktop/tests/test_validation_scripts.py:1092-1110` explicitly states that the six `syn-*` positives have no held-out boundary and separately pins their `REFERENCE_TAIL_STARTS`. Thus PR-MED-005’s amendment remains incomplete for one gate set: its premature-cut and lost-match checks lack a specified expected-boundary source.  
Proposed amendment: Explicitly use the pinned reference-tail mapping for the 50’s positives, define negative classification, and test both rejection conditions on that set.

**PR-MED-009 — The amended run-only fact count includes facts already credited**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:153` counts facts using matching diagonal edges alone, without excluding suspect words. Actual warning credit accumulates on substitution edges too (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:1294`, `:1327-1331`) and excludes suspect words (`:1879-1881`). For example, a material fact `pain left` aligned to two uncovered clinician segments—suspect `pain,` followed by nonsuspect `right`—joins under D1. The second segment already receives old credit; the first gains run-only segment membership. The proposed lookup counts the fact because its sole matching edge reaches the first segment, although the substitution on the second segment already warned it and the suspect word contributes no warning credit. PR-MED-006’s amendment supplies the report path but defines an incorrect “run only” count.  
Proposed amendment: Define the fact count using actual warning-contributing aligned words, including substitution edges and suspect exclusion, and pin this counterexample plus multiple-optimal-alignment cases.

**PR-MED-010 — The live-warning measurement has no defined window input**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:336-338` specifies replay-style retranscription, then promises live-warning windows under both vocabularies. `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/replay_kept.py:363-375` obtains flattened transcript documents, while `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/voice_commands.py:153-170` advances its window index once per `feed()` call and raises only once. Actual live batches are posted together by `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/transcription.py:2495-2552`; transcript segments carry no batch identity (`:340-348`). Feeding individual segments or the entire document produces different results, so the promised magnitude is not defined by the prescribed inputs.  
Proposed amendment: Specify captured or faithfully reproduced live feed batches and the reported window-index convention, or explicitly define and label a deterministic replay surrogate, with tests distinguishing segment grouping from window grouping.

### Round 4 — 2026-10-25
Source: Codex (codex gpt-6-astra medium) plan peer-review

**PR-MED-011 — Role-tail indices are not expected detection boundaries**  
Materiality: build-affecting  
Evidence: `.cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md:164` and `:366` use `reference_tail_start(script)` as every positive’s expected boundary, making PR-MED-008’s amendment incorrect for `heldout-3`. For `h3-02`, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/validation.py:639` supplies role-tail index **10**, while `desktop/tests/test_consultation_boundary.py:135` pins the expected boundary at **9**; `h3-09` similarly has tail **6** and boundary **5** (`validation.py:646`, `test_consultation_boundary.py:136`). This distinction is intentional: `desktop/tests/test_validation_scripts.py:948-953` checks role-tail equality separately from fixture-boundary equality. Consequently, a candidate that correctly detects these boundaries is rejected as cutting too early, and the lost-match comparison uses the wrong target.

- **PR-LOW-001** (LOW, regression, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/models.py:2748`): Negative omission anchors pass the new caller guard and can make `flagged_segments` raise instead of returning its anchor set. `NoteWarning` accepts `SourceCoords(-1, 0, 0)`; its validator checks the warning code/severity, and `GeneratedNote` does not validate warning coordinates. With one wholly uncovered clinician segment and that warning, Python resolves `segments[-1]`, then `joined_run` raises `ValueError`. A more negative index raises `IndexError` earlier. The former chooser simply returned the anchor set. This contradicts Round 14’s assertion that unresolved anchors are guarded. — Evidence: `"anchor < end"` followed by `"segments[anchor].speaker"` and `"runs.update(joined_run(document, anchor, covered=covered, end=end))"` (`ui/models.py:2748–2752`); `"if not 0 <= i < end <= len(segments):"` (`note.py:1859`); warning validation at `note.py:817` and `note.py:947`. ### Round 16 - 2026-10-26 - Stage-4 (Phase 1: the joined-run unit, fix re-review and documents slice), independent cross-family codex peer review (pass stage-4.p1)

*(Recorded by the composer verbatim from `.cursor/loops/stage-4-peer-r16.log`, last `### Round 16` header; the peer ran read-only. Peer pass stage-4.p1, peer round 2 of cap 5.)*


- Round status: Closed (0 pending)
- Source: independent cross-family codex peer review
- Reviewer: codex gpt-6-astra (medium)
- Scope: Working tree versus HEAD `8d0caf7`; Round 15’s coordinate fix, its permitted callers and tests, and the nine specified document slices. Static inspection only; no files written, tests executed or network commands run.

#### Findings

- **PR-LOW-001** (LOW, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:1801`): The coordinate resolver still interprets a negative last-word index relative to the end. On a three-word segment, `SourceCoords(0, 0, -2)` passes both guards and returns the first two words rather than `None`. Warning coordinates permit this (`note.py:778`, `note.py:817`). In the existing `hedged_source` caller, if that segment holds a hedge, the returned words make the condition true, but the subsequent `range(0, -1)` adds no marked positions; the invalid warning also bypasses `hedge_marks_unhedged` (`validation.py:2005`). Thus the class fix remains incomplete, although this case does not falsely credit a fact. The new resolver pin covers negative segment and first-word indices, but no negative last-word index (`test_note.py:2747`). — Evidence: `"if coords.first_word_index < 0 or coords.last_word_index >= len(words):"` followed by `"return words[coords.first_word_index : coords.last_word_index + 1]"`; the caller uses `"if marked and segment_hedged(document, coords.segment_index):"`. Recommendation: Fix-now — require `0 <= first_word_index <= last_word_index < len(words)` before slicing; pin negative last-word and reversed intervals, alongside valid intervals, and the invalid hedge-warning caller behavior. /fix decision: Applied (verified VALID, `materiality=non-build severity=LOW surface=production rec=Fix-now`: the guard read the first index's sign and the last index's upper bound only, so a negative last index sliced from the end and a reversed interval returned `()`, not None; `note._words_for_coords` now resolves only `0 <= first_word_index <= last_word_index < len(words)` in a segment that exists — every valid interval's result is unchanged, and `NoteSpan` already refuses a negative or reversed span, so no assertion caller can change. ## Follow-Up Continuation Notes
- If run 7 fails on R1 alone (unmarked confident errors, or R1 over its ceiling): the larger-model plan is the next candidate — never a rule or predicate change.
- If `heldout-4` fails on R3 after the widening: the practitioner's own phrases (Deferred) and the author-model qualifier are the first things to read; a further widening is a new dated decision with another blind set.
- If the budget is unmet at n: record it; the unit does not change for the budget (Excluded: V2).

---
*Plan saved to: .cursor/plans/plan-validation-run7.md*
*To resume in a new session: open a fresh Agent (Ctrl+I), run /start-session, then run /load-plan*
*Sections marked (Only if relevant) can be omitted when genuinely not applicable, but never omit decisions, constraints, verification steps, or integration knowledge that a fresh session would otherwise have to rediscover.*
*For lightweight plans, plans with fewer than 5 tasks, or plans created without a preceding `/explore` discussion, sections with no real content should be marked `N/A — simple feature` rather than padded with placeholder structure.*
*State-once convention (new plans): state each cross-section contract — a critical constraint, design decision, invariant, or other normative rule — once in its canonical section, and have every other section point to it rather than restate it. Scope guard: this governs cross-section normative duplication only — the Task Specification Standard (in `/create-plan` and `/review-plan`) and task-local detail are untouched, and a plan recording `fast/medium involved` in its Executor tier may keep extra restatement where it helps the fast executor. It applies to new plans as they are written; never retroactively rewrite an existing plan to conform.*
